The functions
filt_{k}(\bm{u})
and
nab_{k}(\bm{u})
are the functional versions of (
2.9
) and (
2.76
). Their argument,
\bm{u}
, is the argument of optimization problem (
3.1
) and their outputs are what would be (
2.9
) and (
2.76
) if they were evaluated at
\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1}=\bm{u}
.

By construction, the KMFCA-D/I algorithms avoid the problem discussed earlier and illustrated in
3.1
as one can see in the following example:

Example 3.1
.

(Illustration of the risks associated with the linearization of locally concave constraints)

One considers the following theoretical RTO problem:

\displaystyle\phi(u,\bm{y}):=\

\displaystyle y_{(1)},

\displaystyle y_{p(1)}:=\

\displaystyle 5-14u+8u^{2},

\displaystyle y_{(1)}:=\

\displaystyle 0.2-2u+5u^{2},

\displaystyle g(u,\bm{y}):=\

\displaystyle y_{(2)},

\displaystyle y_{p(2)}:=\

\displaystyle-2+7u-29u^{2}+32u^{3},

\displaystyle y_{(2)}:=\

\displaystyle-2+9u-35u^{2}+40u^{3},

where
u\in[0,1]
. Figure
3.2
illustrates what these functions are.

Figure 3.2:
Costs and constraints functions of the plant and the model.

The minimum of the nominal model is
u_{0}=0.2
. This is therefore the point at which one initializes the MFCA and KMFCA. The simulation results are shown on Figure
3.3
.

: MFCA,

: KMFCA,

: Unfeasible area,

: Optimum.

Figure 3.3:
Simulation results

One can observe that KMFCA provides much better performances than MFCA. Indeed, MFCA significantly violates the constraints, and if one observes its curve
log_{10}(|u_{k}-u_{p}^{\star}|)
, one can see that it is roughly shifted by five iterations to the right. To explain these observations, a detailed analysis is presented in Figures
3.4
and
3.5
.

:
\phi_{k}(u)
,

:
g^{c}_{k}(u)
,

:
g_{k}(u)
,

:
g_{k}(u)
unused,

:
u_{k}\rightarrow u_{k+1}
,

:
u^{\star}_{k+1}
,

Figure 3.4:
Details of MFCA iterations.

Figures
3.4
clearly illustrates the problem that MFCA faces. At iterations
k=0
and
k=1
the function
g_{k}
is concave at
u_{k}
and is therefore linearized, hence the magenta and light red curves for these two iterations. Since
g_{k}
is ignored in favor of
g^{c}_{k}
, MFCA can aim at the points
u_{1}^{\star}
and
u_{2}^{\star}
that are not feasible according to
g_{k}
and thus generate risky iterations. Hence the large constraint violation.

On the other hand, when KMFCA is used, although the constraint
g_{k}
is linearized for the choice of
u_{1}^{\star}
and
u_{2}^{\star}
, its satisfaction is imposed at the points
u_{1}
and
u_{2}
; hence, better management of constraints and the absence of violation

Figure 3.5:
Details of KMFCA iterations (Legend: see Figure
3.4
).

However, to validate these improvements, one needs evidence that the satisfaction of the equilibrium and stability conditions is not lost.

3.2.1
Effects on the equilibrium condition

To show that the satisfaction of the equilibrium condition is not lost, an analysis of the geometrical properties of Problems (
2.1
) and (
3.1
) updated at
\bm{u}_{k}
in the vicinity of
\bm{u}_{k}
is required. Indeed, it is clear that the fact that (
3.1
) has twice as many constraints as the plant (
2.1
) implies that, globally, the feasible domains of these two optimization problems are different, i.e.:

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{p}(\bm{u})\leq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

\displaystyle\not\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{k}(\bm{u})\\
\bm{g}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k}))\end{array}\right)\leq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},\quad\forall\bm{u}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}.

However, this does not mean that around
\bm{u}_{k}
they are not similar. In the previous chapter is was shown that, by forcing these two problems to have similarities at
\bm{u}_{k}
(see (
2.17
) and (
2.33
)), the validity of the conditions of equilibrium and stability of MFCA-D/I can be enforced. So, one chooses to analyze these two optimization problems at
\bm{u}_{k}
through their cones of feasible directions (CDF) at that point. These two cones, called
\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}
for the plant and
\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}
for the updated model, can be defined mathematically as follows:

\displaystyle\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}:=\

\displaystyle\left\{\bm{d}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}\ |\ \bm{g}_{p}(\bm{u}_{k})\leq\bm{0},\ \nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}<0\text{ if }g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=0,\forall i\right\},

(3.9)

\displaystyle\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}:=\

\displaystyle\big\{\bm{d}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}\ |\ \bm{g}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k})\leq\bm{0},\ \nabla_{\bm{u}}g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}<0\text{ if }g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=0,\text{ and }...

\displaystyle\ \ \nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k}))|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}<0\text{ if }g_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})...

\displaystyle\ \ (\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k})|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=0,\forall i\big\},

(3.10)

and they can be geometrically interpreted as the set of directions along which one can move starting from a feasible point
\bm{u}_{k}
while remaining in the feasible domain. If one can prove that
\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}=\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}
, then it would mean that locally the feasible and non-feasible areas of (
2.1
) and (
3.1
) are the same, i.e.:

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{p}(\bm{u})\leq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{k}(\bm{u})\\
\bm{g}_{k}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k}))\end{array}\right)\leq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},\quad\forall\bm{u}\in\mathcal{B}(\bm{u}_{k},r\rightarrow 0).

With the following Lemma, it is shown that this is always the case.

Lemma 3.1
.

Consider the optimization problem of the plant given by
(
2.1
),
and the one of the model updated at
\bm{u}_{k}
given by (
3.1
), such that equalities (
2.17
) and (
2.33
) are true. Then:

•

The cone of feasible directions of (
2.1
) at
\bm{u}_{k}
:
\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}:=\text{\eqref{eq:3___2_CFDk}}
, and the cone of feasible directions of (
3.1
) at
\bm{u}_{k}
:
\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}:=\text{\eqref{eq:3___2_CFDk_prime}}
, are equal:

\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}=\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}.

(3.13)

•

If Problem (
2.1
) is LICQ at
\bm{u}_{k}
, then Problem (
3.1
) is MFCQ (Mangasarian-Fromovitz Constaints Qualification) at
\bm{u}_{k}
. .

Proof.

Starting by proving the first statement. To do so, the following three observations are made:

\displaystyle g_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+\underbrace{filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k})|_{\bm{u}_{k}}}_{=\bm{0}}=g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}.

(3.14a)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k}))|_{\bm{u}_{k}}

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=\left(\nabla_{\bm{u}}filt_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}(\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k})\hskip-2.84526pt+\hskip-2.84526ptfilt_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}\right)\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}},

\displaystyle\qquad\qquad\qquad\qquad=filt_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}.

(3.14b)

\displaystyle\text{\eqref{eq:3___21}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{c}_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=\

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}},

(3.14c)

By combining (
3.14a
), (
3.14b
), (
3.14c
), and by construction
filt_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}>0
, the following is stated:

\displaystyle\begin{array}[]{r@{}lcr@{}l}g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=&0,&\Rightarrow&g^{c}_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k})|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=&0,\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}>&0,&\Rightarrow&\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{k(i)}(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k}))|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}>&0.\end{array}

So the definition (
3.10
) of
\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}
can be reduced to:

\displaystyle\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}=\big\{\bm{d}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}\ |\ \nabla_{\bm{u}}g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}<0\text{ if }g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=0,\forall i\big\}.

(3.14f)

As equalities (
2.17
) are assumed to be true, i.e.
g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=\ g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}
and
\nabla_{\bm{u}}g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}
,
the definition of
\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}
, given by (
3.14f
), can be reformulated as:

\displaystyle\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}=\big\{\bm{d}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}\ |\ \nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}^{\rm T}\bm{d}<0\text{ if }g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}=0,\forall i\big\}=\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}.

(3.14g)

Which proves the first statement of the lemma.

Now one can easily prove the second statement. To prove that Problem (
3.1
) is MFCQ at
\bm{u}_{k}
for all
\bm{u}_{k}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}
, one must show that
\forall\bm{u}_{k}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}:

\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}\neq\varnothing
. By definition, the CFD of a LICQ problem is never empty
[
15
]
. So, if (
2.1
) is LICQ at
\bm{u}_{k}
, then
\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}\neq\varnothing
. As according to (
3.14g
),
\text{CFD}_{\text{p,k}}=\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}
, one can conclude that:
\text{CFD}_{\text{k}}\neq\varnothing
.
So the Problem (
3.1
) is always MFCQ at
\bm{u}_{k}
.

∎

On the basis of this local geometric similarity between Problems (
3.1
) updated at
\bm{u}_{k}
and (
2.1
), it can be shown that KMFCA-D/I converges only on a KKT point of the plant, see Theorem
3.1
. Also, the equilibrium condition of KMFCA-D/I can be identified, see Theorem
3.2
.

Theorem 3.1
.

If KMFCA-D/I converges on the limit value
\bm{u}_{\infty}:=\underset{k\rightarrow\infty}{\lim}\bm{u}_{k}
, then

\exists\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}
such that
(\bm{u}_{\infty},\bm{\lambda})
is a KKT point of the model updated at
\bm{u}_{\infty}
, and such that
(\bm{u}_{\infty},\bm{\lambda}_{p})
is a KKT point of the plant with
\bm{\lambda}_{p}=\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}
, where
(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime})\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}}
and
\bm{\lambda}:=[\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\rm T},\ \bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\rm T}]^{\rm T}
.

Proof.

Thanks to Lemma
3.1
, one knows that Problem (
3.1
) is MFCQ at
\bm{u}_{k}

\forall k
, and therefore at
\bm{u}_{\infty}
. So, according to
[
15
]
:

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{\infty}:=\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\phi_{\infty}(\bm{u})\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}(\bm{u})\leq\bm{0},

\displaystyle\phantom{\bm{u}_{\infty}:=\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\phi_{\infty}(\bm{u})\quad\text{s.t.}\quad}\bm{g}_{\infty}\big(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty})\big)\leq\bm{0},

(3.15)

\displaystyle\hskip-0.28453pt\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\exists\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\\
\bm{g}_{\infty}(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty}))|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\end{array}\right)\leq\bm{0},\\
\bm{\lambda}^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\\
\bm{g}_{\infty}(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty}))|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\end{array}\right)=0,\\
\bm{\lambda}\geq\bm{0},\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}+\bm{\lambda}^{\rm T}\left(\hskip-2.84526pt\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty}))|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\end{array}\hskip-2.84526pt\right)=\bm{0},\end{array}\right.

where
\bm{\lambda}:=(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\rm T},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime\rm T})^{\rm T}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}
,
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}}
are the Lagrange multipliers associated to the constraints
\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}
, and
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}}
are the Lagrange multipliers associated with the constraints
\bm{g}_{\infty}(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty}))|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}
. Noting that:

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{\infty}\big(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty})\big)\big|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}},

(3.23)

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}\big(\bm{u}_{\infty}+filt_{\infty}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{\infty})\big)\big|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle\big(\nabla_{\bm{u}}filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}(\bm{u}_{\infty}-\bm{u}_{\infty})+...

\displaystyle filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\big)\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}},

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}.

(3.24)

and that

\displaystyle\text{\eqref{eq:3___21}}\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}},

(3.25)

system (
3.2.1
) can be re-written as follows:

\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{\eqref{eq:3___8}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___9_kbrgcs}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___10_iuachngb}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___15_sgljxbhv}}\end{array}\right\}\Rightarrow\exists\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\leq\bm{0},\\
\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=0,\\
\bm{\lambda}\geq\bm{0},\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}+\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\bm{0}.\end{array}\right.

(3.26)

In addition, as
\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\leq\bm{0}
,
\bm{\lambda}\geq\bm{0}
and
filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}>0
:

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\sum_{i=1}^{n_{g}}\lambda^{\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}+\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle 0,\ \forall i,

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\lambda^{\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{(i)}g_{\infty(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle 0,\ \forall i,

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\

\displaystyle 0.

(3.27)

So, (
3.26
) can be rewritten:

\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{\eqref{eq:3___9}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___10_b}}\end{array}\right\}\Rightarrow\exists\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}:\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\leq\bm{0},\\
\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=0,\\
\bm{\lambda}\geq\bm{0},\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}+\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\bm{0}.\end{array}\right.

(3.28)

As KMFCA-D/I enforces the equalities (
2.17
), one can replace
(\bm{g}_{\infty},\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\infty},

\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{\infty})|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}
with
(\bm{g}_{p},\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{p},\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{p})|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}
, and as

\displaystyle\bm{\lambda}\geq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)\geq\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

(3.29)

it is observed that
\bm{u}_{\infty}
is a KKT point of the plant; indeed:

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\text{\eqref{eq:3___10}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:2___11_SimpleApproach_SameCones}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___11}}\end{array}\right\}\Rightarrow\exists\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}:

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\leq\bm{0},\\
\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\bm{0},\\
\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)\geq\bm{0},\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}+\big(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\big)^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}=\bm{0}.\end{array}\right.

Therefore, if KMFCA-D/I converges on
\bm{u}_{\infty}
, then
(\bm{u}_{\infty},\bm{\lambda})
is a KKT point of the updated model, and
\big(\bm{u}_{\infty},\bm{\lambda}_{p}=(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}+filt_{\infty}|_{\bm{u}_{\infty}}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime})\big)
is a KKT point of the plant.
∎

Theorem 3.2
.

(Equilibrium condition of KMFCA-D/I)

Consider the plant optimization problem given by (
2.1
), its solution (
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
), the RTO algorithms: KMFCA-D/I, and the following notations:

•

The functions
\phi_{\star}
and
\bm{g}_{\star}
are the functions of the model updated at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
, and Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
refers to Problem (
3.1
) updated at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
,

•

\bm{N}_{\star}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}\times n_{b}}
is a matrix whose
n_{b}
columns form an orthonormal basis of the null space of the Jacobian of active constraints of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
, i.e.
\forall i
such that
g_{\star(i)}
is an active constraint of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{\star(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\bm{N}_{\star}=\

\displaystyle\bm{0}.

(3.37)

•

\bm{N}_{p}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}\times n_{c}}
is a matrix whose
n_{c}
columns form an orthonormal basis of the null space of the Jacobian of active constraints of Problem (
2.1
) at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
, i.e.
\forall i
such that
g_{p(i)}
is an active constraint of Problem (
2.1
)
{}|_{\star}
at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}g_{p(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\bm{N}_{p}=\

\displaystyle\bm{0}.

(3.38)

Then, the equilibrium condition of KMFCA-D/I is that

\bm{N}_{p}^{\rm T}\left(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\phi^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{g}}\lambda_{p(i)}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}g^{c}_{\star(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\right)\bm{N}_{p}>0.

(3.39)

By default this condition is satisfied because
\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\phi_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}>0
and
\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}g^{c}_{\star(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\geq 0

\forall k
.

Proof.

For the equilibrium to be possible, the 1st and 2nd order optimality conditions of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
must be satisfied at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
.

Part 1 – 1st order optimality conditions:

By definition:

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}:=\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\phi_{p}(\bm{u})\quad\text{s.t.}\quad\bm{g}_{p}(\bm{u})\leq\bm{0},

(3.40)

\displaystyle\text{\eqref{eq:3___wegrw} }\Leftrightarrow\

\displaystyle\exists\bm{\lambda}_{p}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}}\text{ tel que }\left\{\begin{array}[]{l}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\leq\bm{0},\\
\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\bm{0},\\
\bm{\lambda}_{p}\geq\bm{0},\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\bm{0}.\end{array}\right.

As KMFCA-D/I implies that the functions of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
satisfy both (
2.17
) and
(
2.33
):

\displaystyle\left.\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\leq\bm{0}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:2___11_SimpleApproach_SameCones}}\\
\text{\eqref{eq:3___21}}\end{array}\right\}

\displaystyle\Rightarrow

\displaystyle\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\\
\bm{g}_{\star}\big(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+filt_{\star}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})\big)\big|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\end{array}\right)=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\\
\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\end{array}\right)\leq\bm{0}.

\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}
is defined as a variable which one separates into two sub-vectors
(\bm{\lambda}^{\prime},\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime})\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}}
, as follows:

\bm{\lambda}:=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\\
\bm{\lambda}^{\prime\prime}\end{array}\right):=\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\lambda}_{p}\\
\bm{0}\end{array}\right)\geq\bm{0}

(3.52)

As
\bm{\lambda}_{p}\geq\bm{0}
, it is clear that
\bm{\lambda}:=
(
3.52
) implies that
\bm{\lambda}\geq\bm{0}
. So:

\displaystyle\bm{\lambda}^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\\
\bm{g}_{\star}\big(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+filt_{\star}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})\big)\big|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\end{array}\right)=\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=0.

In addition, as
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}
, and
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}
,
the following is observed:

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{0},

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\nabla_{\bm{u}}\phi^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\bm{\lambda}_{p}\\
\bm{0}\end{array}\right)^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{c}\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\\
\nabla_{\bm{u}}\bm{g}_{\star}\big(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+filt_{\star}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})\big)\big|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\end{array}\right)=\

\displaystyle\bm{0}.

The union of equations (
3.2.1
)-(
3.52
)-(
3.2.1
)-(
3.2.1
) shows that if
(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\bm{\lambda}_{p})
is a KKT point of the plant, then

(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\bm{\lambda}=[\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T},\ \bm{0}^{\rm T}]^{\rm T})
is a KKT point of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
.

Part 2 – 2nd order optimality condition:

As Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
is MFCQ at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
, the 2nd order optimality condition at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
is, the existence of a set of KKT multipliers
\bm{\lambda}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{2n_{g}}
such that the 1st order optimality conditions are satisfied and such that (
[
15
]
):

\bm{N}_{\star}^{\rm T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\mathcal{L}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\bm{N}_{\star}>\bm{0},

(3.59)

where
\mathcal{L}_{\star}
is the Lagrangian of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
, i.e.

\mathcal{L}_{\star}(\bm{u})=\phi_{\star}(\bm{u})+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{g}}\lambda^{\prime}_{(i)}g^{c}_{\star(i)}(\bm{u})+\lambda^{\prime\prime}_{(i)}g_{\star(i)}\big(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+filt_{\star}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})\big).

(3.60)

With the candidate
\bm{\lambda}:=
(
3.52
), for which the validity of the 1st order optimality conditions have been proved in part 1, (
3.60
) becomes:

\mathcal{L}_{\star}(\bm{u})=\phi^{c}_{\star}(\bm{u})+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{g}}\lambda_{p(i)}g^{c}_{\star(i)}(\bm{u}).

(3.61)

According to Lemma
3.1
, one can replace
\bm{N}_{\star}
by
\bm{N}_{p}
. Indeed, the fact that
CFD_{\star}=CFD_{p,\star}
implies that the all the gradients of the active constraints of Problems (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
and (
2.1
)
at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
belong to the same subspace of
\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}
. Hence:
\bm{N}_{\star}=\bm{N}_{p}
. One can therefore conclude that the condition (
3.59
) is:

\bm{N}_{\star}^{\rm T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\mathcal{L}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\bm{N}_{\star}=\bm{N}_{p}^{\rm T}\left(\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\phi^{c}_{\star}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}+\sum_{i=1}^{n_{g}}\lambda_{p(i)}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}g^{c}_{\star(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}}\right)\bm{N}_{p}.

(3.62)

As
\forall k

\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}\phi^{c}_{k}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}>0
and
\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}g^{c}_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}\geq 0
, this condition is valid at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
.

Conclusion

In summary, it is shown that the couple
(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\bm{\lambda}=[\bm{\lambda}_{p}^{\rm T},\ \bm{0}^{\rm T}]^{\rm T})
satisfies the 1st and 2nd order optimality conditions of Problem (
3.1
)
{}|_{\star}
. The existence of this point is sufficient to show that KMFCA-D/I guaranties the satisfaction of the equilibrium condition in all circumstances.
∎

At this stage, it has been demonstrated that KMFCA-D/I provides the same guarantees as MFCA-D/I with respect to the validity of the equilibrium conditions. Now, moving on to the stability conditions.

3.2.2
Effects on the stability condition

As KMFCA-D/I are purely iterative methods, the solution of problem (
3.1
) is a function
\bm{sol}^{\prime}
of the point
\bm{v}
around which the most recent experiments have been conducted. In more mathematical terms, (
3.1
) can be written as:

\displaystyle\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v}):=\

\displaystyle\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\widetilde{\phi}^{c}(\bm{u},\bm{v}),

\displaystyle\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{c}(\bm{u},\bm{v})\leq\bm{0},

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad}\widetilde{\bm{g}}\big(\bm{v}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{v}),\bm{v}\big)\leq\bm{0},

(3.63)

where it is recalled that

\displaystyle\phi_{k}^{c}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\widetilde{\phi}^{c}(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{k}),

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}^{c}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{c}(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{k}),

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{k}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{g}}(\bm{u},\bm{v}_{k}).

(3.64)

Then, a Theorem similar to Theorem
2.3
can be formulated:

Theorem 3.3
.

If:

•

A purely iterative RTO method using (
3.63
) is used,

•

For any correction point
\bm{v}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}
the following equalities are true:

\displaystyle\widetilde{\bm{g}}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}=\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{c}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}=\

\displaystyle\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{v}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\widetilde{\bm{g}}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}=\nabla_{u}\widetilde{\bm{g}}^{c}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}=\

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\bm{g}_{p}|_{\bm{v}},

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}=\

\displaystyle\nabla_{u}\phi_{p}|_{\bm{v}},

\displaystyle\nabla^{2}_{\bm{u}\bm{u}}\widetilde{\phi}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}+\sum_{i=0}^{n_{g}}\left[\lambda^{\prime}_{(i)}\widetilde{g}_{(i)}|_{\bm{v},\bm{v}}\right]>\

\displaystyle 0,

\displaystyle\forall\bm{\lambda}^{\prime}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{g}+},

where the functions
(\widetilde{\phi},\widetilde{\bm{g}})
are defined by (
3.64
).

•

For any correction point
\bm{v}\in\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}
, Problem
3.63
is such that
\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v})
is unique.

Then, the following statements are true:

A.

\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v})
is
\mathcal{C}^{0}
and piecewise-
\mathcal{C}^{1}
in the neighborhood of the KKT points of the plant;

B.

If
\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet}
is a KKT point of the plant and
\delta\bm{v}
is a vector in
\amsmathbb{R}^{n_{u}}\backslash\bm{0}
, then
\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet})=\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet}
and the value of the directional derivative of
\bm{sol}^{\prime}
:

\displaystyle\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{v},\delta\bm{v}):=\

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\delta\bm{v}}{\|\delta\bm{v}\|}\right)^{\rm T}\lim_{\begin{smallmatrix}\alpha\to 0&\\
\alpha>0\end{smallmatrix}}\frac{\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v}+\alpha\delta\bm{v})-\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{v})}{\alpha},

(3.65)

\displaystyle\Big(=\

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\delta\bm{v}}{\|\delta\bm{v}\|}\right)^{\rm T}\nabla_{v}\bm{sol}^{\prime}|_{\bm{v}}\frac{\delta\bm{v}}{\|\delta\bm{v}\|},\quad\text{ si $\bm{sol}^{\prime}$ est $\mathcal{C}^{1}$ en $\bm{v}$}\Big).

\text{is: }\left\{\begin{array}[]{ll}\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet},\delta\bm{v})<1,&\text{if $\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet}$ is a minimum of the plant},\\
\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet},\delta\bm{v})>1,&\text{if $\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet}$ is a maximum of the plant},\\
\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet},\delta\bm{v})\gtrless 1,&\text{if $\bm{v}_{p}^{\bullet}$ is a saddle-point of the plant},\end{array}\right.\qquad\quad

(3.66)

C.

If
\bm{v}_{p}^{\star}
is a minimum of the plant and
\delta\bm{v}
is a vector orthogonal to the set of active constraints of the plant at
\bm{v}_{p}^{\star}
such that
\nabla^{S+}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\star}\delta\bm{v})<-1
, then the Lagrangian of the plant must be at least two times more convex than the one of the model in the direction
\delta\bm{v}
, i.e.:

\delta\bm{v}^{\rm T}\nabla^{2}_{\bm{uu}}(\mathcal{L}_{p}|_{\bm{v}_{p}^{\star}}-2\mathcal{L}|_{\bm{v}_{p}^{\star},\bm{v}_{p}^{\star}})\delta\bm{v}>0\ \Rightarrow\ \nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{v}_{p}^{\star},\delta\bm{v})<-1.

(3.67)

Proof.

The proof of this Theorem is very similar to the one of Theorem
2.3
. The only significant differences are that (i) the names and the number of constraint are not the same, and (ii) the constraints of Problem (
3.63
) are not LICQ but MFCQ. This only affects the fact that in this case the KKT multipliers are no longer functions of
\bm{v}
.
∎

When this theorem is applicable one can link two successive iterations
\bm{u}_{k}=\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+\delta\bm{u}
and
\bm{u}_{k+1}
with the following equation:

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k+1}=\

\displaystyle\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+\delta\bm{u}_{k}),

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\bm{sol}^{\prime}(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})+\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\delta\bm{u}_{k})\delta\bm{u}_{k}+\mathcal{O}(\|\delta\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}),

\displaystyle=\

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}+\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\delta\bm{u}_{k})\delta\bm{u}_{k}+\mathcal{O}(\|\delta\bm{u}_{k}\|^{2}),

(3.68)

\displaystyle\Leftrightarrow

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k+1}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}=\

\displaystyle\nabla^{S\prime}(\bm{u}_{p}^{\star},\delta\bm{u}_{k})(\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star})+\mathcal{O}(\|\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}\|^{2}).

(3.69)

Clearly, one can notice the great similarity between (
3.68
)-(
3.69
)
and (
2.47
)-(
2.48
). Therefore, an argument similar to the one used in Chapter
2
starting from equation (
2.48
) can be used to demonstrate two things. First:

Theorem 3.4
.

If the conditions of applicability of the Theorem
3.3
are satisfied, and if:

•

The problem is 1-D (
n_{u}=1
);

•

The function
sol^{\prime}
is
\mathcal{C}^{1}
at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
;

•

The filter
K>0
is applied as follows:

u_{k+1}=u_{k}+K(sol(u_{k})-u_{k});

Then, any filter adaptation strategy that satisfies:

\displaystyle K_{k}<\

\displaystyle\frac{2}{1-\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}^{S}_{k}},

\displaystyle\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}^{S}_{k}:=\

\displaystyle\left(\frac{\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}}{\|\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}\|}\right)^{\rm T}\frac{\bm{u}^{*}_{k+1}-\bm{u}^{*}_{k}}{\|\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}\|},

\displaystyle\text{if }\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}_{k}^{S}<1.

(3.70)

guarantee that the stability condition is satisfied.

Proof.

The proof of this theorem is identical to the proof of Theorem
2.4
where one would replace the function
sol
by
sol^{\prime}
.
∎

Second, the “optimal” filter that maximizes the convergence speed on
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
when one is close to
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
is:

\displaystyle K^{\star}_{k}=\

\displaystyle\max\left\{K_{o},\frac{1}{1-\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}^{S}_{k}}\right\},

\displaystyle\text{if }\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}_{k}^{S}<1.

(3.71)

Finally, if (
2.55
) and (
3.70
) are compared, then it can be seen that
\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}^{S\prime}_{k}
and
\utilde{\text{\hskip 0.43057pt$\nabla$}}^{S}_{k}
have the same definitions. So, this filter adaptation strategy, based on the functions
filt_{k}
and
nab_{k}
, guarantees that KMFCA-D/I validates the stability conditions. Moreover, as the optimization problem (
3.1
) specifies that the filtered point based on the
filt_{k}
and
nab_{k}
must be feasible according to the updated model, the application of the filter does not present the danger shown in Figure
3.1
. Finally, all the observations and graphical interpretations made in the previous chapter for MFCA-D/I are applicable to KMFCA-D/I. Now, looking into how the performances of KMFCA-D/I can be improved.

3.3
Management of irrelevant constraints

Convexifying the concave constraints enforces the satisfaction of the equilibrium condition, however this action can also have negative effects on the performance of the algorithm. The following example illustrates this point.

Example 3.2
.

(Bypassing an inactive constraint)

Considering the following theoretical RTO problem:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}4\\
2\end{array}\right)^{\rm T}\bm{u}+\frac{1}{2}\bm{u}^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}8&0\\
0&4\end{array}\right)\bm{u},

\displaystyle\phi(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0.1\\
0.1\end{array}\right)^{\rm T}\bm{u}+\frac{1}{2}\bm{u}^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&0\\
0&2\end{array}\right)\bm{u},

\displaystyle g(\bm{u}):=g_{p}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle-2+\left(\begin{array}[]{c}6\\
10\end{array}\right)^{\rm T}\bm{u}-\frac{1}{2}\bm{u}^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}20&0\\
0&36\end{array}\right)\bm{u},

where
u_{(1)}\in[0,1]
and

. Figure

illustrates those functions.

The first thing one notices on Figure

\bm{u}
). This is something that in practice would be totally unacceptable and would immediately disqualify MFCA from any real implementation. Ignoring this problem, a second observation is that convexifying the constraint induces a spreading of the constraint over a large domain of the input space which in turn induces a progressive (i.e. slow) bypass.

Proceeding to the detailed analysis of the KMFCA iterations based on Figure
3.9
. It can be observed that each iteration respects the boundaries on the inputs as well as the constraint
g_{k}
. So, apparently the filter-based constraints solves the main problem of MFCA. However, KMFCA suffers from the same weakness as MFCA in its inability to rapidly bypass the constrained sector. It should be recalled that this weakness is present despite the fact that (i) there is no modeling error on the constraint, and (ii) the constraint is not active at
\bm{u}_{p}^{\star}
.

Example
3.1
confirms the critical importance of checking the feasibility of the updated model at
\bm{u}_{k+1}
at the iteration
k
. Furthermore, it clearly disqualifies MFCA from any practical application by showing that even the boundaries on the plant inputs can be violated.
However, the systematic convexification of the set of concave constraints at
\bm{u}_{k}
can give disproportionate importance to constraints that would otherwise be irrelevant in the decision making. To avoid such performance loss, one idea could be to linearize a concave constraint at
\bm{u}_{k}
if, and only if, it has an effect on decision making, i.e. if it is predicted to be active at
\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\star}
or at
\bm{u}_{k+1}
. The following procedure to identify the constraints to convexify is proposed:

function
f

[A]=

CaID
(\bm{g}_{k},n_{g},\bm{u}_{k+1},\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1},b)

(3.72)

Inputs:

\bm{g}_{k}
: the constraint functions of the model updated at
\bm{u}_{k}
;
n_{g}
: the number of constraints;
\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\star},\bm{u}_{k+1}
: the optimum of the updated model and the filtered point; and
b
: a scalar to be chosen smaller than
0
and which tends to
0
.

Outputs:

A
: the indices of the concave constraints that should influence the decision making (the ones that should be convexified).

A=[\ ]
;

for

i=1:n_{g}

if

\nabla_{\bm{uu}}^{2}g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k}}\not\geq 0

&&

\big(g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k+1}}\geq b

||

g_{k(i)}|_{\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\star}}\geq b\big)

A=[A;i]

end

end

If one applies this idea to KMFCA-D/I, then two new methods that avoid performance losses due to constraint bypass are obtained. These two methods are called
modifier-filter-(active)curvature adaptation direct with filter-based constraints (KMFCaA-D)
and
modifier-filter-(active)curvature adaptation indirect with filter-based constraints (KMFCaA-I)
and are detailed hereafter:

Alg. 7:
KMFCaA-D

Initialization.
Provide
\bm{u}_{0}
,
\bm{K}_{0}(=0.1)

\bm{a}

for

1)

Measure

on the plant.

\phi

Compute

(\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1},K_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1})
as follows:

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1}:=\

\displaystyle\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\phi^{c}_{k}(\bm{u}),

\displaystyle\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad\bm{g}_{k}(\bm{u})\leq\bm{0},

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad}\bm{g}_{k}\big(\bm{u}_{k}+filt_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k})\big)\leq\bm{0},

(3.73)

\displaystyle K_{k}:=\

\displaystyle filt_{k}(\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1}),

(3.74)

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k+1}:=\

\displaystyle\bm{u}_{k}+K_{k}(\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1}-\bm{u}_{k}),

(3.75)

\displaystyle\text{where}:\qquad\quad

\displaystyle\hskip-17.07164ptfilt_{k}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\left\{\begin{array}[]{l@{}l}\hskip-5.69054ptK_{o},&\text{if }k=0,\\
\hskip-5.69054ptK_{k-1},&\text{if }nab_{k}(\bm{u})<1\ \&\ k>0\ \&\ |\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}|\leq\bm{a}\\
\hskip-5.69054pt\max\left\{K_{o},\frac{1}{1-nab_{k}(\bm{u})}\right\}\hskip-2.84526pt,&\text{if }nab_{k}(\bm{u})<1\ \&\ k>0\ \&\ |\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}|>\bm{a},\\
\hskip-5.69054pt1,&\text{otherwise.}\end{array}\right.

\displaystyle\hskip-8.53581ptnab_{k}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\frac{\left(\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}\right)^{\rm T}\left(\bm{u}-\bm{u}^{*}_{k}\right)}{\|\bm{u}_{k}-\bm{u}_{k-1}\|^{2}}.

(3.80)

5)

Identify
with the function
CaID
the set
A
of constraints that are concave at
\bm{u}_{k}
and active at
\bm{u}_{k+1}
or
\bm{u}_{k+1}^{\star}
.

6)

If

A\neq\varnothing

recompute

(\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1},K_{k},\bm{u}_{k+1})
with:

\displaystyle\bm{u}^{\star}_{k+1}:=\

\displaystyle\operatorname{arg}\underset{\bm{u}}{\operatorname{min}}\quad\phi^{c}_{k}(\bm{u}),

\displaystyle\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad g_{k(i)}(\bm{u})\leq 0,

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,..,n_{g}\}\backslash A

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad}g^{c}_{k(i)}(\bm{u})\leq 0,

\displaystyle\forall i\in\{1,..,n_{g}\}\cap A,

\displaystyle\phantom{\qquad\text{s.t.}\quad}\bm{g}_{k}\big(\bm{u}_{k}+K_{k}(\bm{u})(\bm{u}-\bm{u}_{k})\big)\leq\bm{0},

\displaystyle K_{k}:=\

\displaystyle\text{\eqref{eq:3___44_New_filter}},\quad\bm{u}_{k+1}:=\text{\eqref{eq:3___45_New_point}}.

(3.81)

7)

Stop
if
\bm{u}_{k+1}\approx\bm{u}_{k}
and return
\bm{u}_{\infty}:=\bm{u}_{k+1}
.

end

3.4
The plant’s constraints

Before concluding this chapter, it is important to emphasize that KMFCA and KMFCaA reduce the risks of violating plant constraints by enforcing that the point applied at each iteration to be feasible according to the updated model. However, reducing these risks does not mean that these risks are absent. The following example illustrates this point.

Example 3.4
.

(Plant constraints can be violated)

Consider the following theoretical OTR problem:

\displaystyle\phi_{p}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0.3\end{array}\right)\right)^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&1\\
1&2\end{array}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0.3\end{array}\right)\right),

\displaystyle\phi(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0.5\\
0.6\end{array}\right)\right)^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}2&-1.1\\
-1.1&2\end{array}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}0.5\\
0.6\end{array}\right)\right),

\displaystyle g_{p}(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle 0.3-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)\right)^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1&0\\
0&1\end{array}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1\\
0\end{array}\right)\right),

\displaystyle g(\bm{u}):=\

\displaystyle 0.75-\frac{1}{2}\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1.2\\
0.1\end{array}\right)\right)^{\rm T}\left(\begin{array}[]{cc}1.7&0.51\\
0.51&1.7\end{array}\right)\left(\bm{u}-\left(\begin{array}[]{c}1.2\\
0.1\end{array}\right)\right),

where

and

. Figure

It can observed that all the methods converge on the optimum of the plant and that on the path they take to reach this optimum:

•

MFCA violates the boundaries on
u_{(1)}
and
u_{(2)}
. This kind of problem has already been discussed in Example
3.2
.

•

KMFCA and KMFCaA violate the plant constraints at one iteration. The reason for this violation is simply that these two methods do not use the actual constraint functions of the plant but only those of the updated model. Figures
3.14
and
3.15
show what are the updated-model-based optimization problems that KMFCA and KMFCaA use at each iteration. What can be observed on these figures is that
\bm{u}_{k}
(the point in grey) is systematically feasible according to the updated model. What one can conclude is that the feasibility according to the updated model is not sufficient to ensure the feasibility of the plant. Which is quite logical.
